I don't know, it just turns into five more before I can pull myself back.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain situations or activities because of your worries or fears?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess I do. I've... been skipping dance night a lot lately. And I haven't been going to my mentoring sessions, either. It's just... too much to deal with. And at work, it's... I don't know, I check things so many times that I get behind on my support tickets. Other people have had to cover for me, which... I don't like, but I just can't seem to get through it fast enough anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that you consider irrational or unwanted, but that keep repeating in your mind?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, the thoughts I have are just... about things I actually need to do. They're not irrational, they're just... work. I don't have unwanted thoughts like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or on edge?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah. I guess that's... kind of why I'm here. I just feel... wound up, I guess. Almost all the time. It's like my body is just... bracing for the next bad thing to happen. Even when I'm just sitting at my desk, or... trying to go to dance night. I just can't seem to... relax.\n\nDoctor: Do you often experience physical tension, such as tight muscles, that doesn't seem to go away?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, my neck gets a little stiff from looking at the screen all day, but... that's just normal work stuff. I don't think it's anything unusual.\n\nDoctor: Are your worries spread across many areas of your life or are they mainly focused on one or two things?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess they're kind of spread out. It's not just one thing. Like, it used to just be about my condo, but... now it's just a lot of things. I worry if my cousin is mad at me, or... if I'm going to mess up on a support call at work. And then I just start thinking about how I'm ever going to save money or... just move forward with things. It just kind of jumps around.\n\nDoctor: Have your worries significantly interfered with your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I suppose so. At work, I've... I've been falling behind on my tickets. I just keep double-checking things that I know how to do, and... I guess some of the others have had to pick up my slack. And... I haven't been going to dance night lately. Or my mentoring sessions. I just... I feel too overwhelmed to go, so I just stay home.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that are repetitive, unwanted, and intrusive, such as fears of contamination or harm?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I worry a lot, like I said, but... it's not like that. I don't really have thoughts like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that are intrusive and difficult to control, and that are not just worries about everyday things?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. It's just... mostly the normal things I already mentioned. I don't think I have thoughts like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that are intrusive, and difficult to control?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess so. It's... it's hard to stop them. Like, I'll tell myself I already checked a spreadsheet or... or sent an email, but... the thoughts just keep looping. One \"what if\" just... turns into five more. And I just... I can't really pull myself back from it very easily.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your worries or fears are mostly related to social situations, like interacting with people or being judged?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I don't like being judged, but... my worries are just about everything, not just people.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this way, with worries about many different things, for more than 6 months?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah. It's been... probably about nine months. That was when... well, when my condo loan started moving toward foreclosure. So... since then, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worries are causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I suppose so. It's... it's getting in the way of things. It's mostly just... work stuff. Like, I'll tell myself I already checked a spreadsheet, or sent an email, but... the thoughts just kind of keep looping. I get one 'what if' in my head, and... I don't know, it just turns into five more before I can pull myself back.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain situations or activities because of your worries or fears?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess I do. I've... been skipping dance night a lot lately. And I haven't been going to my mentoring sessions, either. It's just... too much to deal with. And at work, it's... I don't know, I check things so many times that I get behind on my support tickets. Other people have had to cover for me, which... I don't like, but I just can't seem to get through it fast enough anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that you consider irrational or unwanted, but that keep repeating in your mind?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, the thoughts I have are just... about things I actually need to do. They're not irrational, they're just... work. I don't have unwanted thoughts like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or on edge?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah. I guess that's... kind of why I'm here. I just feel... wound up, I guess. Almost all the time. It's like my body is just... bracing for the next bad thing to happen. Even when I'm just sitting at my desk, or... trying to go to dance night. I just can't seem to... relax.\n\nDoctor: Do you often experience physical tension, such as tight muscles, that doesn't seem to go away?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, my neck gets a little stiff from looking at the screen all day, but... that's just normal work stuff. I don't think it's anything unusual.\n\nDoctor: Are your worries spread across many areas of your life or are they mainly focused on one or two things?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess they're kind of spread out. It's not just one thing. Like, it used to just be about my condo, but... now it's just a lot of things. I worry if my cousin is mad at me, or... if I'm going to mess up on a support call at work. And then I just start thinking about how I'm ever going to save money or... just move forward with things. It just kind of jumps around.\n\nDoctor: Have your worries significantly interfered with your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I suppose so. At work, I've... I've been falling behind on my tickets. I just keep double-checking things that I know how to do, and... I guess some of the others have had to pick up my slack. And... I haven't been going to dance night lately. Or my mentoring sessions. I just... I feel too overwhelmed to go, so I just stay home.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that are repetitive, unwanted, and intrusive, such as fears of contamination or harm?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I worry a lot, like I said, but... it's not like that. I don't really have thoughts like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that are intrusive and difficult to control, and that are not just worries about everyday things?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. It's just... mostly the normal things I already mentioned. I don't think I have thoughts like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that are intrusive, and difficult to control?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess so. It's... it's hard to stop them. Like, I'll tell myself I already checked a spreadsheet or... or sent an email, but... the thoughts just keep looping. One \"what if\" just... turns into five more. And I just... I can't really pull myself back from it very easily.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your worries or fears are mostly related to social situations, like interacting with people or being judged?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I don't like being judged, but... my worries are just about everything, not just people.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this way, with worries about many different things, for more than 6 months?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah. It's been... probably about nine months. That was when... well, when my condo loan started moving toward foreclosure. So... since then, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worries are causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I suppose so. It's... it's getting in the way of things. At work, I've started falling behind on my support tickets... just because I keep double-checking simple tasks so much. My coworkers have had to... pick up the slack lately. And... I don't know, outside of work, I've skipped a few dance nights and... some mentoring sessions I usually do. I just... I feel too overwhelmed to show up.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worries are worse than what most people would worry about in the same situations?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess so. I mean, the condo makes sense, but... my worry doesn't really stay on that anymore. It just... jumps around. \n\nLike, I'll start worrying about whether my cousin is upset with me... or if I'm going to make a mistake on a support call. And then... I don't know, I just start stressing over how I'll ever save money or... move forward with my life at all. So... yeah, probably.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worries are realistic, or do you think you might be worrying more than you need to?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know. I guess... maybe I worry more than I need to. It's hard to tell. It just... it feels real to me when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that are intrusive, and difficult to control, and that are not about real-life problems?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't really know about them not being real-life problems. They're usually about normal things, I think. Like work. But... yeah, they are hard to stop. \n\nEven when I tell myself I've already... checked a spreadsheet, or sent an email... the thoughts just keep looping. It's like... one \"what if\" turns into five more... before I can really pull myself back.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced significant worry or anxiety for most of your life?\n\nPatient: No, not my whole life. Just... recently, I guess. It started about nine months ago... when the, um, the condo loan thing started heading toward foreclosure. That's when it got bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that are not about real-life problems and are difficult to control?\n\nPatient: Um... I think I just said... they are about real-life things. Just... work and the condo. I don't really have thoughts about things that aren't real. They're just... hard to control, like I said.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
